There's no specific thought or anything, just... dread.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific that happens or something you think about right before you wake up feeling scared?\n\nPatient: I really don't think there's anything specific. Like, I don't remember any nightmares or anything right before I wake up. It's just that sudden jolt, you know? But speaking of waking up, my neighbor has this dog, I think it's a beagle mix or something, and it just howls. Not barks, but that long, drawn-out a-wooooo sound. He leaves it out on the balcony all day while he's at work. I feel so bad for it, honestly. I tried leaving a note on his door once, just being polite like, 'Hey, your dog seems lonely,' but he took it down and never said anything. Some people just shouldn't have pets if they don't have the time for them. It's not fair to the animal. But anyway, no, I don't remember thinking about anything right before I wake up scared. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the scared feeling you wake up with might be related to something that's been on your mind lately?\n\nPatient: I mean... maybe? My mind just never shuts up lately. I just keep going over every little thing I ever did wrong, you know? Like with my patients at work, or with my family. Just every single mistake on a loop. I had this one patient, Mr. Abernathy, and he loved those little strawberry candies, the ones wrapped in the strawberry print wrappers? But he wasn't supposed to have them because of his swallowing issues. His daughter would bring them anyway. She drove this massive white SUV, like one of those ones that takes up two parking spaces, and she always parked right in the ambulance loading zone. It drove the transport guys crazy. I saw a tow truck almost take it once, but he let her off. Tow truck drivers make really good money, actually. My cousin's boyfriend does that and he just bought a jet ski. \n\nBut... what was I saying? Oh, my mind. Yeah, I just lay there thinking about how I'm a completely worthless aide. I just feel like... like God is mad at me. Like He's actually angry with me because I'm not protecting people better. I should be doing more to keep everyone safe, but I'm just useless. I can't even keep my own houseplants alive, honestly. I bought a fern from the grocery store because the tag said it was easy, and it was completely brown in a week. They say you're supposed to mist them with a spray bottle, but who has time for that? But yeah, I just feel like God is punishing me for being so worthless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless all the time, or is it just when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: It's pretty much all the time. Not just when I'm trying to sleep. Even during the day, I just keep going over every little thing I ever did wrong... with my patients, or with my family. Like my sister, she just had a baby. A little boy. I bought him this yellow onesie with a duck on it, but it was a size too small. Babies grow so fast, you know? You buy newborn size and they wear it for like two days. Baby clothes are so expensive anyway. I usually shop at the thrift store on 4th street because they do half-off tags on Tuesdays. You can get really good sweaters there if you don't mind a little pilling. I have this little electric fabric shaver thing that takes the fuzz off, but it takes AA batteries, and I always forget to buy them when I go to the dollar store. The dollar store by my apartment always smells like cinnamon, no matter what time of year it is. It's kind of weird. \n\nBut... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right. No, it's all day long. I just can't stop feeling like I'm a totally worthless aide. It just sits heavy on me that God is mad at me, like He's watching me all day, angry because I'm not protecting people better. It never really stops.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's something specific that you've done that makes you think God is mad at you?\n\nPatient: I mean... I don't know if there's one specific thing, but... I just know I'm messing up at work. Even when my supervisor looks over my care notes and says they're totally fine, I just know deep down that I missed something. Like, I know one of my clients got hurt because of me, and they're just... they're just not telling me yet. My supervisor, Brenda, she always wears these really loud, clunky bracelets. Like, bangles, you know? So whenever she's coming down the hall to check on us, you can hear her a mile away. Jingle, jingle, jingle. It's actually kind of helpful because you always know when she's around. She brought in donuts last week for someone's birthday. I think it was Gary from maintenance. He's retiring next month and moving to Florida. A lot of people move to Florida when they retire. I guess because of the weather? But honestly, the humidity there would ruin my hair. I went to Orlando once when I was a kid and my hair was just a giant frizz ball the whole time. We went to this alligator park and they let you hold a baby one, and its skin felt really weird. Like a bumpy purse. \n\nBut... yeah, what was I saying? Oh, about God being mad. Yeah, I just know I did something wrong. I know someone got hurt because of my mistakes, even if Brenda says the paperwork is fine. They're just waiting to tell me. That's why God is so angry. I'm just not keeping them safe.\n\nDoctor: Was there a particular event or time when you started feeling like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Well, it started... I guess it's been going on for about a year and two months? That's when my agency changed my schedule. They put me on rotating overnight shifts and gave me all this extra paperwork to do. I hate paperwork. They use these blue folders for the new intake forms, and the plastic on them always cracks at the spine. My friend Jessica, she works at an office supply store, and she says the clear ones hold up way better. She gets an employee discount, which is nice. We used it to buy a bunch of colored gel pens once. I love writing with gel pens, especially the sparkly ones, but they always smear if you're left-handed. I'm right-handed, but my uncle is left-handed, and he always gets ink all over the side of his hand when he writes. He works construction, though, so his hands are always kind of dirty anyway. He just built this huge deck on the back of his house. It's made of that composite wood stuff that never rots. \n\nBut... wait, what was the question? Oh, when it started. I think about dying a lot. Mostly when I'm driving home in the morning after my shift. I just stare at the road and wish I could just... not wake up, or just disappear. My car is this old Honda Civic, and the heater takes forever to warm up. So in the mornings, especially in the winter, it's just freezing in there. I have this thick wool blanket I keep in the backseat that my grandmother crocheted for me. It's green and brown, kind of ugly colors, honestly, but it's really warm. She used to crochet all the time while watching those daytime soap operas. The ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having an evil twin. I don't know how people keep up with those storylines. They've been on the air for like forty years. My neighbor watches them too, and she has this tiny little Chihuahua that barks at the TV every time the doorbell rings on the show. It's so annoying. I can hear it through the wall sometimes when I'm trying to sleep during the day. \n\nBut... oh, what was I saying? Right, driving home. I just think about disappearing. I really feel like everybody would be safer without me around. Like I wouldn't be able to hurt anyone or mess up anymore if I was just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times or days when you don't feel worthless?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It never really goes away. I just... I keep going over every little thing I ever did wrong, you know? With my patients, or with my family. Like, my mom... I forgot to call her back last week. She always leaves these really long voicemails. Usually about her garden. She grows these huge tomato plants, the heirloom kind? They get really heavy and you have to tie them up to wooden stakes or they just fall over into the dirt and rot. I had to wear them to my cousin's wedding last summer and it was like ninety degrees out. The wedding was outside by this lake, and the mosquitoes were just eating everybody alive. They had those citronella candles everywhere, but they smell so bad, like fake lemons and burning plastic. I got melted wax on my shoe and it never came off. \n\nBut... sorry, what did you ask? It's one of those big ugly plastic ones with a red second hand that ticks really loud. My grandpa used to have a clock kind of like that in his hallway, but his was made of dark wood. He had to wind it up every Sunday with this little brass key. He kept the key in a ceramic bowl that looked like a duck. I actually broke the duck's beak off when I was little, and I tried to fix it with Elmer's glue. But it just dried all white and crusty. Elmer's glue smells so weird, kind of like sour milk. It just feels like I'm a completely worthless aide, and God is just so mad at me for not protecting people better. I went to Orlando once when I was a kid and my hair was just a giant frizz ball the whole time. We went to this alligator park and they let you hold a baby one, and its skin felt really weird. Like a bumpy purse. \n\nBut... yeah, what was I saying? Oh, about God being mad. Yeah, I just know I did something wrong. I know someone got hurt because of my mistakes, even if Brenda says the paperwork is fine. They're just waiting to tell me. That's why God is so angry. I'm just not keeping them safe.\n\nDoctor: Was there a particular event or time when you started feeling like you're worthless?\n\nPatient: Well, it started... I guess it's been going on for about a year and two months? That's when my agency changed my schedule. They put me on rotating overnight shifts and gave me all this extra paperwork to do. I hate paperwork. They use these blue folders for the new intake forms, and the plastic on them always cracks at the spine. My friend Jessica, she works at an office supply store, and she says the clear ones hold up way better. She gets an employee discount, which is nice. We used it to buy a bunch of colored gel pens once. I love writing with gel pens, especially the sparkly ones, but they always smear if you're left-handed. I'm right-handed, but my uncle is left-handed, and he always gets ink all over the side of his hand when he writes. He works construction, though, so his hands are always kind of dirty anyway. He just built this huge deck on the back of his house. It's made of that composite wood stuff that never rots. \n\nBut... wait, what was the question? Oh, when it started. Yeah, about a year and two months ago, when they switched my shifts to overnights and added all that paperwork. That's when I really started feeling so... useless.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that you would be better off dead or that your family would be better off without you?\n\nPatient: I do... I think about dying a lot. Mostly when I'm driving home in the morning after my shift. I just stare at the road and wish I could just... not wake up, or just disappear. My car is this old Honda Civic, and the heater takes forever to warm up. So in the mornings, especially in the winter, it's just freezing in there. I have this thick wool blanket I keep in the backseat that my grandmother crocheted for me. It's green and brown, kind of ugly colors, honestly, but it's really warm. She used to crochet all the time while watching those daytime soap operas. The ones where everyone is always coming back from the dead or having an evil twin. I don't know how people keep up with those storylines. They've been on the air for like forty years. My neighbor watches them too, and she has this tiny little Chihuahua that barks at the TV every time the doorbell rings on the show. It's so annoying. I can hear it through the wall sometimes when I'm trying to sleep during the day. \n\nBut... oh, what was I saying? Right, driving home. I just think about disappearing. I really feel like everybody would be safer without me around. Like I wouldn't be able to hurt anyone or mess up anymore if I was just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times or days when you don't feel worthless?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It never really goes away. I just... I keep going over every little thing I ever did wrong, you know? With my patients, or with my family. Like, my mom... I forgot to call her back last week. She always leaves these really long voicemails. Usually about her garden. She grows these huge tomato plants, the heirloom kind? They get really heavy and you have to tie them up to wooden stakes or they just fall over into the dirt and rot. We used to use old pantyhose to tie them up because regular string would cut into the stems. Pantyhose are so uncomfortable though. I had to wear them to my cousin's wedding last summer and it was like ninety degrees out. The wedding was outside by this lake, and the mosquitoes were just eating everybody alive. They had those citronella candles everywhere, but they smell so bad, like fake lemons and burning plastic. I got melted wax on my shoe and it never came off. \n\nBut... sorry, what did you ask? Oh, right, days I don't feel worthless. No, I don't have days like that. Because I just sit there replaying all my mistakes, and it just feels like I'm a completely worthless aide. Like God is just so mad at me for not protecting people better. I just can't stop thinking about all the ways I didn't protect them.\n\nDoctor: Are there any moments, no matter how brief, when you don't feel worthless?\n\nPatient: Like I just said... no, not even for a minute. Even if I just have a brief moment, like... well, we get these fifteen-minute breaks at work, right? But they aren't even really fifteen minutes because the clock in the break room is fast. It's one of those big ugly plastic ones with a red second hand that ticks really loud. My grandpa used to have a clock kind of like that in his hallway, but his was made of dark wood. He had to wind it up every Sunday with this little brass key. He kept the key in a ceramic bowl that looked like a duck. I actually broke the duck's beak off when I was little, and I tried to fix it with Elmer's glue. But it just dried all white and crusty. Elmer's glue smells so weird, kind of like sour milk. I can't even drink milk anymore, it always upsets my stomach. Unless it's in cereal, but then you have to eat it really fast before it gets soggy. Frosted Flakes are the worst for getting soggy. \n\nWait... what did you just ask me? Something about brief moments? Oh, right. Yeah, no, it's exactly like I was just saying. I just constantly go over every little thing I've ever done wrong with my patients or my family. It never stops. It just feels like I'm a completely worthless aide, and God is just so mad at me for not protecting people better. I can just feel how angry He is.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of worthlessness usually triggered by something specific that happens during your day?\n\nPatient: Not really... it doesn't take a specific trigger. It just happens all the time. Like, even if I'm just doing laundry at work. I have to wash so many sheets and towels for the residents. The laundry room at the facility always smells like really strong bleach. It gives me a headache sometimes. My aunt used to use so much bleach when I lived with her back in high school. She ruined my favorite black t-shirt once. It got these ugly orange spots all over the bottom hem. It was a shirt I got from the county fair. The fair had this huge Ferris wheel that got stuck while my friend and I were at the very top. We were up there for like an hour just swinging in the wind, and I dropped my funnel cake over the side. Funnel cake is too sweet anyway, all that powdered sugar just gets stuck in your throat and makes you cough. I choked on powdered sugar once at a bakery when I was little and threw up right by the counter... \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Triggers? Yeah, no, like I keep trying to explain, nothing specific has to happen. I just sit there and keep going over every single thing I ever did wrong with my patients, or my family. It just plays on a loop in my head. I just feel like I'm a totally worthless aide. And I know God is just so mad at me for not protecting people better. It's just always there, no matter what I'm doing.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for your mistakes?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I do. Especially when I'm alone, like driving in my car or if I'm at a client's house and it's real quiet. My car is this old Honda, the heater doesn't even work right. It just blows cold air for like twenty minutes before it warms up. I tried to get it fixed at this shop down the street, but the mechanic there was so rude. He had grease all over his hands and he didn't even wipe them off before he touched my steering wheel. It left this black smudge that I had to scrub off with a Clorox wipe. I buy those wipes in bulk from Costco. Costco is always so crowded though, especially on weekends. People just block the aisles with their giant carts, trying to get those free samples. Last time I went, they were giving out these little pieces of frozen pizza, but the cheese was barely melted. I hate when cheese isn't melted all the way, it feels rubbery. Like those string cheese sticks they give kids in school lunches. I always traded mine for fruit snacks. The red fruit snacks were the best, but they always put too many yellow ones in the pouch. \n\nWait, what was the question? Punished? Oh, right. Yeah, when it's quiet like that, I hear this voice. It's as clear as if somebody is standing right beside me, telling me I failed them and that I should be punished. But then I look around, and nobody is there. It's just me.\n\nDoctor: Is the voice you hear when it's quiet telling you that you failed and should be punished something that you can hear with your ears, or is it more like a thought in your head?\n\nPatient: Well, it's definitely with my ears. Like, it sounds exactly like someone is talking out loud right next to me. It's so annoying when people talk out loud on their phones in public, you know? Like on speakerphone. I was at the grocery store the other day and this lady was just yelling into her phone in the produce section. The produce section there is always freezing, they keep those water misters on way too long. It makes the lettuce go bad faster, I swear. I bought this head of iceberg lettuce last week and it was already getting brown around the edges by Tuesday. I usually use it for tacos. Tacos are easy to make after a long shift, but the hard shells always break right down the middle when you bite into them. Then all the meat just falls out onto your plate. My dog usually eats whatever falls on the floor. He's a terrier mix, and he sheds everywhere. I have to vacuum the couch like twice a week, and the vacuum belt keeps slipping, it makes this awful burning rubber smell... \n\nWait, sorry, what were you asking me? If it's a thought? Oh, no. No, it's not a thought in my head. Like I said, when I'm alone in my car or if I'm at a client's house and it's real quiet, I hear it with my actual ears. It's a voice, as clear as somebody standing right beside me, telling me I failed them and that I should be punished. But then I turn to look, and there's absolutely nobody there.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the voice when you're in a noisy environment or is it only when it's quiet?\n\nPatient: It's mostly when it's quiet. Like I was saying, when I'm alone in my car or if I'm at a client's house and there's no noise around. If the TV is on, or if there's a lot of people talking, I don't really hear it. I hate when the TV is too loud though, especially those commercials for car insurance. They always play the same one with the stupid jingle over and over again. My uncle used to sell car insurance, actually. He had this little office downtown, right next to a bakery that sold the best bear claws. They were always so sticky, you'd get glaze all over your fingers and the steering wheel if you ate them in the car. That's how my old steering wheel got all gross before I bought a cover for it. The cover I have now is fuzzy, it's supposed to keep your hands warm in the winter, but it just collects dust and dog hair. My dog loves riding in the car, he sticks his head out the window and his ears flap in the wind. But he drools all over the door panel, and it dries into these crusty white spots that are so hard to clean off...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? Noisy places? Oh, right. No, it's not when it's noisy. It's when it's quiet. I'll just be sitting there in the silence, and then I hear it. Clear as day, like someone is standing right beside me, telling me I failed them and I should be punished. And I always look, but nobody is ever there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear is real or just something in your head?\n\nPatient: Well, it feels completely real to me. It sounds just like a real person. Real people are so demanding though, especially my clients. I have this one lady who makes me fluff her pillows exactly three times. If I do it twice, she throws an absolute fit. And she has these silk pillowcases that just slide right off the bed anyway. I don't understand why people like silk sheets, they're always freezing cold when you first lay down. I prefer flannel. I found this really thick flannel set at Target on clearance last year. Target is the worst for that, though. You go in for one thing like paper towels and you end up buying a waffle maker and a bunch of candles. I don't even use the waffle maker because the syrup always drips down the side and gets stuck on the counter, and then you get ants. We had ants so bad in my kitchen last summer, they were coming in right through the electrical outlets... \n\nWait, sorry, what did you ask me? If it's in my head? Right. I mean, I guess it has to be, because like I keep saying, whenever I turn to look, nobody is ever there. But it doesn't feel like a thought. When I'm alone in my car or sitting in a quiet client's house, the voice is as clear as somebody standing right beside me saying I failed them and should be punished. So it sounds totally real, even if there's no one actually standing there.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice you hear ever tell you to do something specific?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really like... giving me instructions or anything. Instructions are so hard to follow sometimes, especially when they come from the agency. They send these memos out in tiny font that you can barely read on your phone. My phone screen is cracked right down the middle anyway because I dropped it in the parking lot at work. The asphalt there is all torn up, huge potholes everywhere. They say they're going to repave it, but they've been saying that since last spring. Spring is my least favorite season because the pollen gets so bad. My car gets completely covered in this thick yellow dust, and if it rains, it turns into this sticky paste that won't even come off in the car wash. I paid for the premium wash last time, the one with the rainbow foam, but it just left streaks all over my windshield. It makes it so hard to see at night when the streetlights hit it... \n\nWait, sorry, what was the question? If the voice tells me to do things? Oh, no. It doesn't tell me to go do anything specific. Like I keep telling you, it's always just that same exact message. When I'm sitting there in the silence at a client's house or driving alone, it's just that voice right next to my ear saying I failed them and I deserve to be punished. It's never giving me a task, just telling me I'm awful and need punishment. And like I said, I turn to look, and I'm totally alone. Every single time.\n\nDoctor: Does the voice you hear ever tell you to hurt yourself or others?\n\nPatient: No, it doesn't say anything like that. It doesn't tell me to hurt anybody. Hurting people is awful, I couldn't imagine doing that. Some of the aides at work are so rough with the clients, though. I saw this one girl, she was trying to get Mr. Henderson into his wheelchair and she basically just yanked him by the arm. His skin is so thin, it bruises if you just look at it wrong. I always try to be gentle, I use this special lotion on his arms that smells like lavender. Lavender is supposed to help you sleep, but it never works for me. I tried those lavender pillow sprays, the little sachets you put in your drawers, even drinking that sleepytime tea with the bear on the box. The tea tastes like dirty bathwater, honestly. And then you just have to get up to pee in the middle of the night anyway. My bathroom floor is tile, and it gets so freezing cold in the winter. I bought this fuzzy bath mat to put down, but the backing started peeling off in the wash and it left all these little rubber crumbs inside my washing machine. It took me hours to wipe them all out with a damp paper towel...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? Hurting people? Oh, right. No, the voice never tells me to hurt myself or anyone else. It's just... like I've been saying, it's always the exact same thing. When I'm driving by myself or sitting in a quiet house, it's just that voice, right there, clear as day, telling me I failed them and that I'm the one who should be punished. But it never tells me to actually do anything. And I always look, and there's never anyone standing there. Just me.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of being a worthless aide affecting your relationships with your family or friends?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really see my friends much lately anyway. But yeah, I guess it does affect things. I just keep going over every little thing I ever did wrong, you know? With my patients, with my family. My sister called me last week to ask if I wanted to go to the movies, but movies are so expensive now. You pay like twenty dollars for a ticket, and then the popcorn is another fifteen. And they don't even use real butter anymore, it's just this weird yellow oil that comes out of a pump. It gets all over your fingers and you can never wipe it off with those cheap little napkins they give you. The napkins are always so thin, they just tear when you try to use them. I prefer the thick paper towels, the Bounty ones, but they cost so much at the grocery store. Everything at the grocery store is crazy right now. Eggs are like five dollars a dozen, and half the time you get them home and one is cracked in the carton. I always try to check them before I buy them, but sometimes the crack is on the bottom where you can't see it...\n\nWait, sorry. What did you ask me? About my family? Right. I just... I keep thinking about all my mistakes. It just feels like I'm a completely worthless aide, and that God is mad at me because I didn't protect people better. Like I should have done more, and now I'm just worthless to everyone.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, work is getting really hard. I have to drive to all these different houses for my visits, and the traffic has been terrible lately. They're doing all that construction on the interstate, and they have those bright orange barrels lined up for miles. Orange is such an ugly color, honestly. My aunt had her kitchen painted orange back in the nineties and it looked like the inside of a pumpkin. She used to make pumpkin pie from scratch for Thanksgiving, but she'd always burn the crust. I don't even like pie, I'm more of a cake person. But grocery store cakes always have that super sweet frosting that just makes your teeth hurt. The frosting is basically just shortening and powdered sugar. I tried to bake a cake once, but I left it in the oven too long because the timer on my stove is broken. The buzzer sounds like a dying smoke detector, so I just took the knob off completely...

Wait, sorry. What were you asking me? About my tasks at work? Right. Yeah, I just... I can't keep up with my care visits safely anymore. It's too much. My supervisor actually had to stop me during a care-plan visit the other day because I was just too overwhelmed and stuck to even finish it. And I'm not doing anything else outside of work either. I've completely pulled away from church, I stopped going to my Zumba classes, and I'm not doing my mentoring anymore. I can barely manage just getting through work and getting back home.

The frosting is basically just shortening and powdered sugar. I tried to bake a cake once, but I left it in the oven too long because the timer on my stove is broken. The buzzer sounds like a dying smoke detector, so I just took the knob off completely...\n\nWait, sorry. What were you asking me? About my tasks at work? Right. Yeah, I just... I can't keep up with my care visits safely anymore. It's too much. My supervisor actually had to stop me during a care-plan visit the other day because I was just too overwhelmed and stuck to even finish it. And I'm not doing anything else outside of work either. I've completely pulled away from church, I stopped going to my Zumba classes, and I'm not doing my mentoring anymore. I can barely manage just getting through work and getting back home."
